TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT 
TROY BOARD OF EDUCATION 
TROY, MICHIGAN 
TO: Board of Education 
FROM:  Richard M. Machesky, Ed.D. 
RE: Agenda Memo for the Organizational Meeting, January 16, 2018 
1. ELECTION OF ACTING CHAIRPERSON 
The first order of business is to e lect an acting chairperson who will 
conduct the meeting until such time as a president has been elected.  
Past practice has been that the previous year’s president conducts this 
part of the meeting. 
2. APPOINTMENT OF TEMPORARY SECRETARY 
 The second order of business is for the acting chairperson  to 
 appoint Judy Holmes to act as temporary secretary until such time as the 
 secretary to the Board has been elected. 
3. ELECTION OF PRESIDENT 
The acting chairperson declares nominations in order for the office o f 
president of the board of education (please note, nominations need not 
be seconded; and a motion and support are made to close the 
nomination.)  Please understand that four votes are required for election. 
4. ELECTION OF VICE PRESIDENT 
The newly elected  president declares nominations in order for the 
office of vice president, following the same procedures as outlined for the 
election of president. 
5. ELECTION OF A SECRETARY 
The president declares nominations in order for the office of secretary, 
following the same procedures as outlined for the election of president. 
6. APPOINTMENT OF TREASURER 
According to Board Policy, the treasurer is appointed annually by the 
Board as a nonvoting officer.  The assistant superintendent of business 
services has served  as treasurer of the Board.  It is my recommendation 
that this procedure continue.

         4. C.        01.16.18 
WHEREAS, there may be in and may, hereafter from time to time, come into the 
hands of Rick West , Treasurer of Troy School District, certain public monies 
belonging to or held for the State, County, or other political units of the State or 
otherwise held according to law, and 
WHEREAS, under the laws of Michigan, this Board is required to provide by 
Resolution for the deposit of all public monies, including tax monies coming into 
the hands of said Treasurer, on one or more banks, hereafter called bank(s) to be 
designated in such Resolution. 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED that said Treasurer, Rick West, is hereby 
directed to deposit and/or invest all public monies, including tax monies now in or 
coming into his hands as Treasurer in his name as Treasurer, into the  following 
depository institutions: 
Bank of America 
Bank of New York 
Charter One Bank 
JP Morgan Chase Bank 
Comerica Bank 
First Merit Bank  
Fifth Third Bank 
Flagstar Bancorp 
MILAF (Michigan Liquid Asset Fund Plus, managed 
By PFM Asset Management, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank) 
Morgan Stanley Smith Barney 
PFM Securities, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank 
PNC Bank 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District may be 
withdrawn by checks signed with a facsimile signature of the Treasurer, Rick West, 
and in emergencies the handwritten signature of the Treasurer, Rick West, or the 
Superintendent, Richard M. Machesky, who may sign on the General Fund account 
and the Payroll account at Bank of America; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belongi ng to the District for the 
accounts established at PNC Bank  for purposes of paying Troy Career Center 
expenses may be withdrawn by checks with the handwritten signature of The Troy 
Career Center Manager, Assistant Manager, or Program Coordinators; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that monies belonging to the District for the account 
established at Comerica Bank for purposes of paying health care and dependent 
care flexible spending claims may be withdrawn by ACH debit to Next Generation 
Enrollment, Inc., or by checks signed with a facsimile signature of the Treasurer, 
Rick West, and 
11
         4. C.        01.16.18 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer, Rick West, may open or close 
accounts with the above -listed institutions, provided it is in the interest of the 
District; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that funds of the District may be invested according 
to Board Policy 5000.06; and 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Treasurer is authorized to combine money 
from more than one fund for the purpose of making an investment authorized by  
subsection (1) (h) of Section 380.1223 of the Revised School Code; and 
THE MAXIMUM AMOUNT TO BE INVESTED with the above institutions is 
regulated by the school code. 
I, ______________________, Secretary of Troy School District, Oakland County, 
Michigan, certify that the Resolution was adopted by said Board at a meeting 
thereof held on the date specified above.

Oakland in the State of Michigan this 16th day of January 2018.

     5. A.   01.16.18 
5.   RECOGNITION  
A.   TSD Mission Moment — Superintendent Student Advisory Board 
  MLK, Jr. Day of Service  
Tonight, as part of our Troy School District Mission Moment, we recognize the 
2017-18 Superintendent Student Advisory Board. The SSAB is made up of 
outstanding student leaders from all four high schools in the District, who come 
together with the expressed mission of leaving the Troy School District better 
than it was when they entered. They are nominated by teachers, counselors and 
administrators and are selected from a group of applicants to serve in the Board. 
Yesterday, these students executed another successful Martin Luther King, 
Junior, Day of Service at Athens High School, where members of the community 
came toget her to learn about the legacy of Dr. King and perform four service 
projects that have a direct positive impact in his name. 
These students helped to plan and carry out the  Day of Service  as well as a 
smaller service project each Spring. They also give inp ut to Dr. Machesky and 
Central Office Staff from a high school perspective and work to encourage 
students at their own schools to participate in these district -wide activities. Our 
SSAB students are leaders on campus and we are proud of what they have 
accomplished. 
Recommended Action:  Invite students up to receive a certificate and 
congratulations from the Board.  
19
      5. B.   01.16.18 
5.  RECOGNITION       
B.    Board of Education Recognition 
Each January, the Troy School District recognizes and celebrates the 
work of a very important group of individuals: The Board of Education.  
A little over three years ago, we embarked on a journey to become a 
World Class School District by the year 2020. That ambitious goal could 
never become a reality without the hard work and dedication of the 
outstanding trustees who make up our Board. Troy is truly fortunate 
to be g overned by Board members who  put students first in all 
decisions and give tirelessly of their time, energy and talents to our 
school community. This community is grateful for all that you and all 
that you give.  
Tonight, we would like to recognize our Board of Education members 
with a gift that represents our Journey to World Class. Each of you is 
receiving a framed print of our four characteristics of World Class. 
These characteristics were developed after studying some of the best 
schools in the country and internationally; and after considerable input 
from our staff, and of course, our Board of Education. 
We hope that this gift serves as a reminder of the impact of your 
leadership in this exciting and important journey. What this Board 
does, truly makes a difference for our s tudents, our staff and our 
community.  
Thank you for all you do.   
Recommended Action:  Present gift to Board members.

Absent:   Philippart 
Mr. Schmidt shared some background about the 
School Finance Research Collaborative.  The state of 
M i c h i g a n  h a s  n e v e r  h a d  a  c o m p r e h e n s i v e  s c h o o l  
adequacy study conducted and local school boards 
are being asked to consider adopting a resolution in 
support of the School Finance Research Collaborative 
and the work its doing to determine the true cost of 
educating Michigan’s public school students. MASA 
and MASB support the work that this group is 
undertaking and would like to send a clear message 
to policymakers in Lansing that this work is critical if 
Michigan’s public schools and the services they 
provide to students are going to be funded in the 
most effective and appropriate manner possible.

WHEREAS, 30 years of research on school district 
leadership has proven a correlation exists between the 
work of school boards and student achievement; and  
WHEREAS, s c h o o l  b o a r d s  i n  h i g h‐achieving districts 
exhibit characteristics and practices that differ greatly 
from those in lower‐achieving school districts regardless 
of socioeconomic status; and  
WHEREAS, the Board of Education of the Troy School 
D i s t r i c t  i s  c o m m i t t e d  t o  g o v e r n i n g  t h e  d i s t r i c t  i n  a  
manner that creates high achievement and positive 
outcomes for students; and   
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that the Board 
of Education of the Troy School District adopts the Board 
of Education Governance Standards published by the 
Michigan Association of School Boards and, in doing so;  
  6 December 19, 2017

Fleming that  
T h e  B o a r d  o f  E d u c a t i o n  o f  t h e  T r o y  S c h o o l  D i s t r i c t  
commits to conducting itself in a manner consistent with 
the Board of Education Governance Standards; and   
Individual members of the Troy School District Board of 
Education commit to conducting him/herself in a manner 
consistent with the Individual Board Member Standards; 
and   
The Board of Education of the Troy School District shall 
e n s u r e  t h e  B o a r d  o f  E d u c a t i o n  G o v e r n a n c e  S t a n d a r d s  
are visible at every meeting, and  
The Board of Education of the Troy School District shall 
visit and revisit the Board of Education Governance 
Standards regularly to ensure their continued influence 
in the board’s work, and  
The Board of Education of the Troy School District shall 
hold itself accountable to these standards, and  
Individual members of the Troy School District Board of 
Education shall address behaviors that are not in 
alignment with the Governance Standards.

WHEREAS, as a result of numerous school shootings 
across the nation, including the tragedies at Columbine 
High School, Sandy Hook Elementary School, and 
s u b s e q u e n t  s c h o o l  s h o o t i n g s  i n v o l v i n g  s t a f f  a n d  
students, the issue of the presence of guns on school 
grounds, at school sponsored activities, or while students 
are en route to or from school, has become an increasing 
concern for Michigan public schools;  
WHEREAS, the Federal Gun Free School Zones Act 
prohibits possession of a firearm in a school zone, but 
exempts an individual licensed to carry a firearm under 
state law. 18 USC 922(q)(2).   
  7 December 19, 2017 
31
Firearms in Schools 
17-104 
Adopted this 19
th day of December, 2017.   
It was moved by Mr. Hauff and supported by Mr. Miletti 
that  
WHEREAS, The Michigan Weapon Free School Zone Act 
similarly prohibits possessi on of a firearm on school 
property, but exempts an individual licensed to carry a 
firearm under state law. MCL 750.237a.  
WHEREAS, the Michigan Concealed Pistol Licensing Act 
prohibits an individual with a concealed pistol license 
from carrying a concealed pistol on school property. MCL 
28.425o.  
WHEREAS, the State of Michigan has not enacted 
legislation expressly prohibiting individuals who possess 
a valid Concealed Pistol License (“CPL”) from openly 
free school zones.”  
WHEREAS, a public school district is tasked under the 
Revised School Code to provide for the safety and 
welfare of its students and to educate students in a safe 
and nurturing environment.   
WHEREAS, the MDE Emergency Plan Tool Kit specifies 
procedures that schools should follow when a weapon is 
present on school grounds such as lockdown, as the 
same may constitute an emergency.  
WHEREAS, in Michigan Gun Owners, Inc. v. Ann Arbor 
Public Schools, 318 Mich A pp 338 (2016) and Michigan 
Open Carry v Clio Area School District, 318 Mich App 356 
(2016), the Michigan Court of Appeals found that school 
districts have the authority to promulgate policies that 
ban the possession of firearms on school property and 
at school-sponsored events.  
WHEREAS, on November 8, 2017, the Michigan Senate 
passed Senate Bills 584-586 which prohibit open carry 
and permit concealed carry on school grounds, day care 
centers, sports arenas, large concert halls, taverns, 
places of worship, hospitals, college dorms or 
classrooms, and casinos for concealed pistol license 
holders with extended training.   
WHEREAS, Governor Rick Snyder has asserted in the 
past that legislation should include an opt out so that 
local school boards may decide whether they want to 
a weapons free school zone.   
WHEREAS, Governor Rick Snyder vetoed legislation 
similar to Senate Bills 584-586 in 2012, because the bill 
did not allow public schools to prohibit persons from 
carrying concealed weapons on their premises. 
  8 December 19, 2017 
32
carrying firearms in
 areas otherwise designated as “gun 
permit weapons
 or opt out to make the school grounds 
NOW THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED AS 
FOLLOWS: 
1. The Board urges lawmakers and the Governor as 
they debate and enact legislation to declare school 
grounds as a safety sensitive zone and to allow local 
school boards to enact policies prohibiting an 
individual who is licensed to carry a concealed pistol 
from carrying a pistol, whether concealed or 
unconcealed, on school grounds, unless statutorily 
required to permit possession of firearms, such as for 
law enforcement personnel.  
2. That copies of this resolution be sent to Governor 
Rick Snyder and the District’s State House 
Representatives and State Senators.

11. BUSINESS 
A.  CONSIDERATION:  Furniture Purchase – 4th and 5th Grade 
Classrooms 
Jennifer Gottlieb, Director of Student Growth, Equity and Accountability, 
led a project team with Workspace Interiors by Office Depot and Building 
Administrators and Staff to evaluate the use and needs for furniture  in 
4th and 5 th grade classrooms .  It is recommended the  
furniture/equipment purchase from the Oakland County America Saves 
Cooperative Purchase Program be approved as stated below.  
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by:   
 Supported by: 
 Be it therefore resolved that the furniture purchase for 4th and 5th grade 
classrooms in the amount of $853,231.22 through Workspace Interiors by 
Office Depot and the Oakland County America Saves Cooperative Purchase 
Program be approved as an expenditure of the 2016 Capital Projects Fund.
